Both memos were developed from the attached project brief and its named source set. 

## Principal recommendations

The absorption memo recommends **one coordinated method-ASK doctrine fold, conditional on the two TBIs first being separately fed and ingested**, while retaining independent provenance and disposition records for each:

* The July 18 TBI’s exact source-role hold should land in `docs/normative-apex.md`: computational source-role eligibility and the possible natural-person terminus both remain explicitly unanswered. 
* The July 28 TBI’s doctrine-ready limbs should land across the existing owners: a positive definition of standing intent, a runtime-realization boundary, and repair against current standing intent. Its Goertzel vocabulary, SMGI analogy, realization scale, and computational-source answer remain external, observation-level, or held. 
* No `governance.md`, bounded-generativity, protocol, lifecycle, storage-plane, diagram, or article change is bundled. Current doctrine already permits computational bounded judgment inside a delegated aperture; that does not settle computational occupancy of the root source role.

The research-plan memo recommends an ecology-root canonical named:

```text
consciousness-free-will_master-context-note.md
```

Its form is a **typed research atlas / master context note**, not a doctrine and not yet a mind map. It separates consciousness, sentience, agency, intention, free will, moral responsibility, institutional authority, personhood, and recourse before testing their relationships. Philosophical reference works likewise treat consciousness and free will as internally plural problem fields rather than interchangeable properties, while collective intentionality introduces agency and attribution questions that cannot be reduced to one individual consciousness. ([Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy][1])

The plan also incorporates current empirical pressure rather than treating older headline experiments as settled verdicts: the 2025 COGITATE adversarial collaboration challenged important predictions of both IIT and GNWT, and the AI-consciousness literature currently offers theory-derived indicators rather than proof that present systems are conscious. ([DOI.org][2])

The framing conclusion preserved across both memos is:

```text
computational bounded discretion     supported
computational root sourcehood        held
consciousness                         ≠ free will
free will                             ≠ intent
moral status                          ≠ authority
recourse                              ≠ incarceration
```

[1]: https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/freewill/ "https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/freewill/"
[2]: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08888-1 "https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08888-1"

COGITATE 2025 is real (Nature 642(8066):133–142, DOI `10.1038/s41586-025-08888-1`, CC BY, free at PMC12137136) and the finding is **mixed on both sides — no refutation, no winner**: IIT's sustained posterior representation passed sparsely (8 of 53 electrodes) while its gamma-synchrony prediction failed; GNWT's offset ignition was absent in all 655 PFC electrodes while category broadcasting partly held. ## Advisor ruling

Claude’s v2 is materially stronger. The anchor ruling is closed, the two-PR structure holds, the lifecycle receipt remains ingestion-only, the consciousness/free-will plan is correctly framed as a survey canonical rather than doctrine, and the cross-tradition plus typed-analogue corrections should stand.

### 1. Dispositions

**July 18 handoff:** ratify `ABSORB`.

After PR-1 lands and the pointer synchronization is complete:

```text
-held-source-role-boundary-ingested.md
→ -held-source-role-boundary-absorbed.md
```

The doctrine being absorbed says that two substantive questions are held open. That does not make the artifact’s disposition `-held`; the artifact’s only operative claim has reached its durable owner.

**July 28 handoff:** ratify absorption of the doctrine-ready limbs, but do not ratify a terminal suffix yet.

Claude is right that the suffix is an output of PR-2’s grading. It is also right that an observation-level landing and a hold are different outcomes. But this clause in its rule is still too permissive:

> “or recorded as declined with no retention”

A declined limb did **not** reach a durable destination. Under the live doctrine, `-absorbed` requires the result to be true of the artifact as a whole, while `-closed` carries mixed claim-level outcomes. The residue rule allows the historical source artifact to survive after absorption; it does not convert an unabsorbed candidate claim into an absorbed one.

The corrected decision rule is:

```text
7-28 → -absorbed

ONLY IF:
- all four doctrine-ready limbs land; and
- both §8 candidate observations also land in a durable method-owned
  surface as explicitly labeled observation-level material, each with:
    candidate generalization
    promotion condition
    project-specific evidence boundary

7-28 → -closed

IF either candidate observation is:
- held
- declined
- routed elsewhere
- no-route
- retained only as source-side evidence or residue
- otherwise left without a new durable owner
```

Because the current smallest-coherent PR-2 does not naturally provide a durable home for the SMGI observation, **`-closed` is the more probable result under the present scope**. That remains a prediction, not a pre-grading ruling. The executor should not distort PR-2 merely to earn `-absorbed`.

### 2. Method sequence

one pair-level closure record

terminal rename of each artifact according to its actual result

The later section insertion made the July 18 memo’s two original placement conditions impossible to satisfy simultaneously. The literal anchor is more specific and preserves the intended conceptual location. 

### 3. Consciousness/free-will P1 sequencing

**Wait for both PR-1 and PR-2 before creating P1.**

Claude is correct that PR-1 is the only strict textual dependency because P1 must quote the two held questions. But P1’s ASK-mapping layer also uses `standing intent`, `runtime realization`, persistence, authorization, and repair—the exact vocabulary PR-2 is about. Creating and snapshotting v1 between the two PRs would knowingly give the new canonical a stale doctrinal base and likely force an immediate corrective version.

Therefore:

```text

→ PR-2
→ pair closure
→ CFW P1 / canonical v1
```

That is an attention and source-of-truth sequencing ruling, not a claim that PR-2 is a formal dependency. It preserves the original project brief’s requirement to plan the canonical before commencing the full research while keeping its ASK mappings downstream of settled method vocabulary. 

### 4. Giant mind map versus relation atlas

Do not choose between them. Use the atlas as the architecture and the giant mind map as its default projection.

Ratify P4 as:

```text
UNDERLYING OBJECT
typed, filterable relation atlas generated from stable canonical IDs

That preserves ASK’s requested artifact while avoiding an unreadable untyped radial diagram. The v2 memo correctly identifies the relation atlas as the better internal model, but it becomes a substitution only if the requested giant overview disappears. With the overview retained as the default view, there is no departure.

## The correction — verified, then adopted
The advisor is right, and I checked the warrant rather than accepting it. `-declined` is enumerated as its own terminal classification at `absorption-discipline.md:87` and appears **nowhere in §Layer destinations**. **Do not create PR-0. Narrowly widen PR-1 to two files.**

The blocker is real: `portable-definition-layer.md` currently states, without qualification, **“Source-of-intent remains human,”** while `bounded-generativity.md` limits the corresponding claim to **the documented implementations** and explicitly says computational delegated judgment does not thereby confer sourcehood. The first is a universal identity restriction; the second is a scoped observation plus a non-entailment. ### Authorized PR-1 scope

```text

before    ## Source of intent is a role, not an artifact
```

- **Source-of-intent remains human.** What the definition layer *means*, and what counts as a governed artifact-or-interpretation worth preserving, is the operator's — not produced by any tool.

**With:**

```

This does three things and no more:

preserves the non-derivability claim
does not answer whether a computational office may be constituted as source

The normative-apex doctrine owns the structural source-role question. Bounded generativity supplies a confirming implementation-scoped non-entailment, but it is a sibling primitive, not the authority for what the apex doctrine may hold open. The corpus should agree without making the structural owner depend on the worked-instance owner.

## Bottom line

The “parallel Phase‑2 thread” is **a separate control-surface maintenance program that you already opened with a `go`**. ## What Phase 2 is doing

Think of `control-surface` as the operating manual for all these repo sessions. Phase 2 is revising that operating manual in three small units.

### P2‑1 — stop workflow loops and improve handoffs

This is the branch currently visible:

```text
protocol/p2-1-throughput-discipline
```

It is intended to prevent the system from becoming trapped in endless checking, correction, and artifact production. Its changes include:

* distinguishing a truly **blocking** problem from a real but **non-blocking** problem;
* requiring a named failure mode before something can stop work;
* terminating correction loops that are no longer adding evidence;
* forcing the executor to reread the finished object before presenting it;
* requiring an internal adversarial check for complex packages;
* separating the actual objects under review from historical files retained only for provenance;
* producing a clearer “ready for review” receipt.

The exact P2‑1 review packet describes the goal as **proportionality/termination plus executor preflight**. 

In ordinary language:

> Make the process rigorous, but stop it from recursively generating more process when nothing decision-relevant is changing.

### P2‑2 — two bounded TBI/handoff amendments

After P2‑1 lands, the parallel thread has two already-scoped handoff/TBI amendments to process and route. This is lifecycle and routing maintenance, not a new doctrine program.

You do not need to decide their substance from scratch. They will come through the usual review and authorization gates.

This final owner-side unit makes the advisor/bootstrap surfaces agree with the revised protocol. It includes the remaining advisor-aperture/carrier conformance and the final bootstrap banner correction.

## Substantive review

### Standing intent

The proposed definition supplies the missing positive content without changing the held source-role boundary:

* standing intent is adopted normative content;
* it has a named scope;
* it remains in force through an applicable governance path;
* it does not require a fresh apex act for every downstream action;
* “standing” as intent status is separated from “standing” as carrier activation.

The four-slot block uses the corpus’s canonical term **artifact of intent**, not a parallel carrier coinage:

```text
source of intent
standing intent
artifact of intent
runtime realization
```

The source-role paragraph landed by PR #156 remains untouched, and nothing in this patch decides whether the source role may be computational or must terminate in a natural person.

The single added non-identity—

```text
realization persistence ≠ continuing authorization
```

—is the right minimum. It captures the authorization failure directly while leaving the other separations in the definitions and repair rule rather than expanding the non-identity field unnecessarily.

### Runtime realization

The `intent-artifacts.md` addition correctly keeps internal goal state outside the artifact-family taxonomy while allowing an explicit representation, policy, configuration, memory entry, or audit to be classified by its own primary artifact role.

The persistent-memory statement is appropriately function-scoped:

```text
the causal function may participate in realization
the memory surface does not thereby become an authoritative
standing-intent carrier
```

That agrees with the live shared rule that private memory is not a durable owner and with the repo’s existing primary-role taxonomy.

### Repair target

The repair section lands the load-bearing rule cleanly:

```text
repair current realization against current standing intent
not merely against the pre-disturbance realization
```

Its three cases remain genuinely distinct:

* **lesion/drift** restores the current authorized target;
* **authorized revision** changes the target through an authorized role acting through governance;
* **candidate correction** preserves possible evidence against the target without silently adopting it or automatically erasing it.

The executor’s preflight correction to the authorized-revision case was necessary and is now correct. Governance is the path and institutional mechanism; the authorized role is the actor. That agrees with the adjacent governance owner’s separation of constituted authority, delegated discretion, revision, adjudication, and recourse.

The phrase “the role authorized to revise that scope” also correctly avoids presupposing a natural-person occupant while the source-role question remains held.

## Substantive review

### Standing intent

standing intent =

standing-intent carrier

### Repair target

not merely against remembered pre-disturbance realization

The three cases are coherent:

## Stage 1 — **CHANGES REQUIRED**

No canonical, snapshot, `_INDEX`, or `_LAYOUT` write is approved.

## BLOCKING 1 — substantive findings have entered the architecture-only version

The canonical says every §VII entry is `LOCATOR-ONLY`, that no source content has been read, and that substantive research begins at P2. But §VII.6 contains a detailed interpretation of COGITATE’s predictions, electrode findings, exploratory analyses, and evidential hierarchy. That is already a bounded empirical research result, not locator metadata. 

The same leakage occurs in two architectural areas:

* `CFW-X-002` and §III.1 pre-sort Davidson and Bratman as consciousness-permissive and Anscombe and Velleman as comparatively restrictive. Labeling the classification “unsourced” discloses it; it does not make it architecture rather than a first-order reading of four theories.
* §V.1 says every v1 pair is typed as an **analogue**, including bounded discretion, while `CFW-Q-005` expressly asks whether that relation is an instance, an analogue, or neither. Calling “analogue” a temporary default still assigns one of the live answer choices.

This creates the failure P1 was designed to prevent: **the skeleton becomes a disguised first research sweep, and the later version sequence can no longer distinguish the container from the first findings.** The accepted plan makes P1 architecture and verified source identity only; substantive sweeps begin at P2. 

### Required correction

1. Remove the content-level COGITATE analysis from v1. §VII may retain locator, edition/version, access, license, publication date, intended lane, and an explicit statement that content-level characterizations await P2. The detailed COGITATE interpretation remains in the plan or working verification evidence until it enters a P2 source row with the appropriate verification depth.

2. Replace the named X‑002 classification with a neutral test obligation:

```text
CFW-X-002  §III.1 — P2 must test, account by account, whether and how an
           intention theory's satisfaction conditions depend on phenomenal
           consciousness, access consciousness, practical knowledge,
           self-understanding, or none of these. v1 assigns no named account
           to either side.
```

The corresponding §III.1 prose should say that Anscombe, Davidson, Bratman, and Velleman will be tested along that dimension—not pre-classified.

3. Remove the default analogue typing. Use:

```text
At v1 the pair register records candidate pairings only. No relation type is
assigned. `ASK-analogue`, `ASK-non-identity`, and `analogue-break` are available
types for later conforming rows; none is a default. Where an open question asks
whether the relation is identity, instance, analogue, or neither, the relation
remains UNCLASSIFIED until evidence and an ASK ruling warrant classification.
```

The bounded-discretion entry should read:

```text
[relation type UNCLASSIFIED — CFW-Q-005 open]
```

## BLOCKING 2 — the survey’s vocabulary guard contradicts its neutrality rule

§0.2 correctly says external literature must not be scored against ASK-internal distinctions: a source that does not draw an ASK distinction has not failed. But §0.6 then says collapsing any listed separation is an error the survey must catch **“in its sources and in itself.”** 

That would allow the survey to treat a source as confused merely because it:

* uses *sentience* and *consciousness* coextensively;
* argues a reduction or identity;
* claims that one status entails another;
* partitions agency, freedom, or personhood differently from ASK.

The special disclosure around `ADV-C1` fixes that problem for one contested pair, but not for the rest of the list. The credible failure mode is **research bent to protect the existing taxonomy**—the exact failure §VI.8 says the ASK-architecture critique must detect.

### Required correction

Replace §0.6’s opening rule with:

```text
These are survey bookkeeping separations and anti-default guards, not prior
verdicts about what no source may argue. The survey must not silently identify
these slots, infer one from another, or attribute an identity without support.
A source may explicitly argue identity, reduction, necessity, or entailment;
record that claim in the source's own terms, with its logical form and evidence,
without treating the source as confused merely for using different vocabulary.
The error is the survey performing or attributing the collapse silently.
```

Retain the existing ADV‑C1 disclosure and the bounded-discretion reading below it.

8. Return a receipt carrying all three final identities and confirming _LAYOUT
   remained unchanged.

Approval expires if:
- either new target now exists;
- the index baseline changes;
- the canonical bytes, target set, index patch, or execution order changes.

P1 closes on the verified receipt.
P2 remains NOT STARTED and requires a separate ASK authorization.

ASK >>

what's the skeleton drift all about, in simple terms? in order to observe n=2, we have to record n=1 somewhere as a candidate

///

You’re right. My prior disposition was wrong: I collapsed **“do not create a new standalone artifact”** into **“do not durably record the observation.”** Those are not the same.

The shared rule is explicit: **an unearned generalization routes to an explicit nomination or hold**—not to no retention.

## Skeleton drift, in simple terms

A skeleton is supposed to be an **empty filing system** for future research:

```text
these are the questions
these are the fields
these are the allowed evidence types
this is how later answers will be recorded
```

**Skeleton drift** happens when, while designing those empty shelves, we quietly start putting answers on them.

That happened several ways in the first CFW draft:

```text
“relation type defaults to analogue”
→ selected one of CFW-Q-005’s live answer options

“these named philosophers fall on this side of the consciousness divide”
→ performed the first research classification

“this literature has this characteristic failure mode”
→ reached a literature-level conclusion before reading the literature

“this is an architectural premise”
→ disguised a provisional finding as part of the container
```

The danger is not merely that the skeleton contains too much prose. The danger is that **P2 inherits those provisional answers as fixed structure**. Research then appears to fill an open map while actually operating inside conclusions chosen before the research began.

The correction was:

```text
premature answer         → UNCLASSIFIED
literature characterization → TEST OBLIGATION
option chosen as default → open question remains open
```

The narrower principle—**a default drawn from the live option set is not neutral**—already has a durable owner in the CFW canonical’s §V.1. 

## The broader pattern does need an n=1 record

The correct disposition is:

```text
new standalone memo       no
private memory             no
method doctrine            not yet
durable held-candidate pointer   yes
```

The exact owner is the **method-ASK grounding note, §Held Method Candidates**. That section says held method-spine candidates are important enough not to remain scratch-only; the grounding note keeps the durable memory that the candidate exists, while full evidence stays in named scratch records. 

So the candidate should be recorded there, pointer-only:

```markdown
- **Skeleton drift / scaffold-to-finding leakage — HELD, n=1.**
  In the Consciousness + Free Will P1 skeleton, an architecture-only version
  repeatedly began encoding first-order findings through defaults drawn from
  live answer options, named-account pre-classifications, literature-level
  characterizations, and provisional findings labeled as architectural premises.

  Candidate failure mode: a schema answers a live question before the research
  begins, after which later research inherits the answer as structure while
  appearing to operate inside an open container.

  Candidate guard: in an architecture-only version, any scaffold rationale that
  chooses from the live option set or characterizes a source, theory, account,
  or literature must remain UNCLASSIFIED or become an explicit TEST OBLIGATION.
  It must not enter as a default or as a premise about the field.

  **Evidence status:** n=1. The several manifestations caught during the three
  P1 review rounds are correlated expressions inside one canonical-construction
  lineage, not multiple independent cases.

  **Promotion condition:** the same causal mechanism recurs in an independently
  produced skeleton or canonical, or ASK expressly rules that the structural
  argument is sufficient before recurrence. At that point, classify the earned
  destination afresh: method doctrine, execution-protocol guard, or a narrower
  local rule.

  **Evidence:** the three CFW P1 Stage-1 proposal manifests and the accepted
  `consciousness-free-will_master-context-note.md` v1.
```

That gives us a durable, searchable **n=1** without pretending it is already a rule.

One precision: if the same issue appears during P2a, that is certainly **new supporting evidence**, but because P2a belongs to the same canonical lineage, it should not automatically be counted as an independent n=2. We would record the recurrence and then judge whether it demonstrates a genuinely new pressure case or merely the same case continuing. The cleanest n=2 would be another independently produced skeleton showing the same scaffold-to-finding leakage.

The bounded next move is therefore a pointer-only `method-ASK_grounding-note.md` v45→v46 proposal plus its byte-identical `_v46` snapshot—not a new evidence memo. The existing review manifests already carry the evidence. The executor performs that write only through ASK’s relay and the configured review gate.

## Skeleton drift, plainly

You're building an empty filing cabinet before doing the research. Labeled drawers, blank folders, rules about what goes where.

Drift is when you start **filing answers while you're still building the cabinet** — and because they arrived as part of the furniture, nobody later notices they were answers.

The four ways it showed up:

- I made "analogue" the **default** relation type. But "analogue" is literally one of the three answers `CFW-Q-005` asks between. Calling it a default rather than an answer changed nothing about what it did.
- I sorted **Davidson, Bratman, Anscombe, Velleman** onto sides of the consciousness question — the exact question the survey exists to investigate.
- I wrote that a literature has a **characteristic failure mode**, having read none of it.
- I labeled provisional conclusions **"architectural premises"**, which disclosed them without changing what they were.

The damage isn't clutter. It's that P2 would open onto a map that *looks* blank, do real research inside it, and confirm conclusions I'd made before starting. The research would feel open and be closed.

The fix in each case was the same shape: **UNCLASSIFIED** where I'd defaulted, **TEST OBLIGATION** where I'd characterized.

The landed method claim is narrower and more precise:

```text
a consequential system requires a locatable apex
regardless of whether its executor is conscious
```

The doctrine then explicitly holds open whether a computational office may occupy the source role. It does **not** claim that every capacity sometimes called “consciousness” is irrelevant to possible source-role occupancy.

The reported Block-style definition of access consciousness—information available for reasoning and rational control—is therefore a **real pressure point**, but not yet a thesis correction.

access-conscious availability may support reasoning and rational control

the normative authority to adopt the governing standard

capacity for deliberation     ≠ authority
rational control              ≠ source-role occupancy
access consciousness          ≠ phenomenal consciousness

the tractable “easy” problems, separable from phenomenal consciousness. That
would support the possibility that governance-relevant functional capacity does
not settle phenomenal consciousness.

UNCLASSIFIED.

## Ruling: proceed with **comprehensive breadth, graduated depth**

No further human ASK decision is needed before assembly.

The proposed framing is right: v2 should map the consciousness field broadly enough that no major family or pressure axis disappears, but it should go deep only where the evidence actually supports depth. The 1.1 MB research corpus is working evidence; the canonical is the structured synthesis, not a dump of everything returned. 

### Evidence treatment

One correction to the proposed numbers:

```text
CONTENT-VERIFIED   46   eligible for primary-text-level source rows
CONTENT-READ       98   eligible for source rows at the declared secondary /
                         mediated depth
LOCATOR-ONLY       98   LEAD only — not a source and supports no survey claim
```

So the ledger is not limited to the 46 `CONTENT-VERIFIED` items. After deduplication, the 98 `CONTENT-READ` items may also receive source rows, but their claims must remain bounded to what was actually read.

Use these rules:

```text
CONTENT-VERIFIED
  direct quotation allowed where exact primary wording was verified
  full claim-supported / claim-not-supported treatment allowed

CONTENT-READ
  paraphrase allowed with the actual intermediary or secondary source named
  no quotation presented as primary wording
  no precision beyond the representation that was read

LOCATOR-ONLY
  LEAD
  locator/access metadata only
  no position card, theory finding, or evidential weight derived from it
```

That follows the live canonical’s own separation between verified sources and availability-only leads. 

## What “comprehensive” should mean here

**Comprehensive coverage, not exhaustive card depth.**

v2 should cover all of §I’s major partitions:

* metaphysical commitments;
* explananda;
* mechanism and theory families;
* pressure nodes on both sides;
* empirical programmes and contested data;
* cross-tradition partitions.

But the raw counts are not quotas. `178 positions` and `68 theory cards` contain lane overlap, duplicate naming, different granularities, and repeated accounts of the same programme.

A defensible result is:

```text
~8–10 empirically active programmes
  full theory cards where the required fields are genuinely supported

major philosophical anchor positions
  full or medium-depth cards where read sources support them

remaining deduplicated positions
  compact reference-depth rows

lead-only positions
  reserved slots or LEAD references, not surveyed positions
```

A full theory card should not be created merely because the lane returned enough prose to fill its fields. If the mechanism, explanandum, evidence-for, evidence-against, or proponent-stated scope cannot be grounded at `CONTENT-READ` or better, mark the card incomplete or keep the item at reference depth.

## Do not pour the remediation outputs wholesale into the canonical

The glossary and datum register are useful, but their raw sizes are not evidence that all of them belong in v2.

Use the glossary for terms whose multiple senses materially affect interpretation in P2a—for example consciousness, access, phenomenal, awareness, reportability, representation, workspace, self-consciousness, and neutrality. Do not import all 130-plus sense distinctions merely because they were discovered.

Use the datum register for empirical results that the canonical actually cites or that multiple theories interpret incompatibly. Each entered datum should carry:

```text
what was measured
paradigm and population
result
which interpretation claims it
what the result does not establish
verification depth
```

The full 62 KB register and raw lane outputs remain working evidence. The canonical should point through stable IDs, not become their verbatim warehouse.

## Provenance corrections stand

## High-level TLDR

**The CFW program is not complete.** The two method handoffs that started it are complete, but the actual consciousness/free-will research program is still early in its planned sequence.

* The July 18 handoff is now **`-absorbed`**: it landed the explicit doctrinal hold on whether a computational office may occupy the source role and whether authority must ultimately terminate in natural persons. 
* The July 28 handoff is now **`-closed`**: standing intent, runtime realization, and repair-target doctrine landed, while its two observation-level claims remained held without a durable landing.
* CFW **P0 and P1 are complete**. The ecology-root canonical exists at v1, but v1 is only the research architecture and verified locator inventory—not the requested comprehensive philosophical recap. 
* **P2a consciousness research and corpus remediation are complete**, but the research has not yet been assembled into canonical v2. The assembled full-object critique and Stage‑1 proposal are still pending. 
* **P2b free will, P2c the consciousness–intention–agency bridge, P2d machine/personhood/recourse, P3 the relationship model, P4 the mind map, and P5 the downstream essays/doctrine applications have not started.**

### The mind map

It was not forgotten or replaced. The ratified design is:

```text
underlying architecture   typed, filterable relation atlas generated from canonical IDs
default projection        one giant all-field zoomable mind map
additional views          filtered, sectional, and printable projections
```

It remains **P4**, after P3 stabilizes the relations. That order matters: building edges before the relationship model is reviewed would turn unclassified possibilities into apparent facts—the visual equivalent of the skeleton drift already caught in P1. 

The likely DS starting point is `diagram-interactive-spine`, which already provides pan/zoom, hover/click, and an inspector. P4 should test whether it can represent many-to-many relations without falsely imposing a hierarchy; otherwise a new Class A interactive relation-atlas pattern should be separately authorized rather than forcing a graph into a spine.

### Does the canonical bring us closer to the computational source-of-intent question?

**Structurally, yes. Substantively, not yet enough to answer it.**

The method work now separates:

```text
source of intent
standing intent
artifact of intent
runtime realization
bounded discretion
selection + closure
governance + recourse
```

It also already establishes that a computational agent may exercise bounded delegated judgment—including selecting or approving images inside a governed AP aperture—without thereby becoming the root source of intent.

The better question is no longer simply “Can a conscious machine be the source of intent?” It is:

> **Under what conditions could a computational office be constituted as the locatable source-of-intent role for a bounded system—able to adopt or authorize the governing standard, maintain and revise standing intent through governance, bind outcomes legitimately, and remain inside an adequate structure of closure, answerability, and recourse?**

Consciousness, free will, intention ownership, moral agency, legal or institutional personhood, and a natural-person terminus remain possible requirements to test. None is yet established as necessary or sufficient.

Also, **seed** and **source of intent** must remain distinct. In SMGI, the seed is an artifact that carries grammar, behavior, loading instructions, and possibly standing intent. It does not constitute the source role merely by successfully rendering an interface. The generated conversation is explicitly not an authority surface.

### The dyad essay and SMGI

The dyad essay v4 already carries the strong core thesis—**the dyad is real, but it sits inside a governance stack**. It should return as v5 only after P3 and an ASK ruling or sharper re-hold, because some current draft language conditionally implies that computational source-role eligibility is already settled. 

SMGI returns downstream for a related but distinct question:

> Is the interface the metaphysical boundary between minds, or a rendered relation among agency, delegation, authority, and perceived personhood?

That remains open. The “app is dead / future is the surface” line stays an ASK product and interface thesis; it does not itself select a theory of consciousness.

## The mind map

**Not forgotten, not substituted, and its shape is already settled** — your "one giant mind map" *is* the default all-field view of a typed filterable atlas. Same object, two layers.

It's behind P3 for a real reason, not ceremony: the atlas is generated from the canonical's stable IDs *and the relations between them*. v1 has the full ID grammar and 18 relation types — and deliberately **assigns no relations at all**. Build the map today and it renders an empty graph with a beautiful legend. **P3 is the phase that produces the edges.**

## Your original five

Item 1 delivered as the container; item 2 deferred as above; items 3, 4, 5 are carried in the canonical as open questions `CFW-Q-004/005/006` so they can't quietly vanish — and answered nowhere.

## Does this get us closer to "what is required for a seed source of intent"?

Not yet. But the sharper thing you asked was **"what is even the correct question here?"** — and the architecture does have a view: **it's probably not one question.** It collapses five that come apart:

```text
1  ROLE TOPOLOGY        what the source role IS, regardless of occupant     ← doctrine answers this
2  OCCUPANCY CONDITIONS what a candidate must satisfy to occupy it          ← HELD OPEN
3  MORAL STATUS         does the occupant matter morally
4  METAPHYSICAL AGENCY  does it have free will / origination
5  ULTIMATE GROUNDING   must the chain end in a natural person              ← HELD OPEN
```

Arguments that a machine can't hold source of intent usually turn out to be arguments about **3 or 4**, applied to **2**. Keeping the five apart is what lets the question stay open honestly instead of being closed by a vocabulary slide.

My recommendation: **P3's first deliverable should be the decomposition, ratified, before any resolution is attempted** — and "the question is malformed as posed" stays a permitted result.

One live distinction worth holding now: **bounded discretion by an agent is already substantially covered** by landed doctrine. The open question isn't whether an agent can exercise bounded discretion — the AP image-approval case is exactly that, and it's settled. It's whether an agent can occupy the *source* role that authorizes the bounds. Different heights in the stack.

## One decision that's yours

Your brief's order is consciousness → free will → bridge. But your live question is *about* the bridge. **Running P2c ahead of P2b would reach your actual question sooner, at the cost of a thinner free-will base under it.** Real trade, and your call.

## The mind map

The mind map is **not forgotten**.

Its settled form is:

```text

## The sharper source-of-intent question

Claude’s strongest addition is that:

> “Could source of intent be computational?” is not one question.

It decomposes into:

```text
1  ROLE TOPOLOGY
   What is the source role, regardless of who occupies it?

2  OCCUPANCY CONDITIONS
   What must a candidate be able and authorized to do to occupy it?

3  MORAL STATUS
   Does the candidate have welfare, patienthood, or moral standing?

4  METAPHYSICAL AGENCY
   Does it possess free will, origination, or ownership of intention?

5  ULTIMATE GROUNDING
   Must the authority chain eventually terminate in a natural person?
```

Public doctrine substantially answers **1**, while **2** and **5** remain explicitly held open.  Questions **3** and **4** are often smuggled into arguments about **2**—for example, treating “not conscious” or “not morally human-like” as automatically meaning “cannot occupy a constituted governance role.”

The stronger eventual question is:

> **Under what conditions may a computational office be constituted as the locatable source-of-intent role for a bounded system—able to adopt or authorize the governing standard, revise standing intent through governance, bind outcomes legitimately, and participate in closure, answerability, and recourse?**

## Bounded discretion versus source of intent

One part is already clear:

```text
a computational agent may exercise bounded discretion
                              ≠
a computational agent is therefore the source of intent
```

An agent can approve images in AP under an inherited brief, governing standard, and bounded aperture. That places it in a delegated judgment role.

The open question is whether it can occupy the higher role that **authorizes the brief, owns or adopts the standard, changes standing intent, closes the system normatively, and answers through governance and recourse**.

Different floors of the stack.

## Seed versus source

A seed is a carrier:

```text
seed    carries the recipe, grammar, instructions, and perhaps standing intent
source  has authority to adopt the recipe, change it, bind through it, and answer for it
```

A seed successfully regenerating an interface does not make the seed the source of intent. Whether a computational entity could become that source is a constitutional and normative question, not merely a capability milestone.

## The only sequencing trade

Claude correctly surfaced one real choice after P2a:

```text
original sequence
  consciousness → free will → bridge

faster route to ASK’s live question
  consciousness → bridge → free will
```

**Recommendation: keep the original sequence.**

The bridge itself asks whether free will equals intent and whether constrained will can be free. Building it before surveying free will would produce a thinner provisional bridge that likely needs rebuilding after P2b. It also reverses ASK’s original instruction: research consciousness and free will comprehensively, then model their relationship. 

So there is no decision to make unless you explicitly prefer **speed to a preliminary answer** over **the comprehensive foundation originally requested**.

  P2c bridge → v4
  P2d machine/personhood/recourse → v5

```

The two original handoffs are complete; their closure record explicitly says the CFW project remains the prospective resolution basis for the still-open source-role questions.  The live index likewise records the CFW canonical as a structured survey that resolves nothing, not as a completed philosophical program.

**This is not a large engineering project unless the automation tail is pulled in too early.**

## High-level TLDR

Ecology already has nearly all the required pieces:

```text
PTX                         where the program came from
folded recommendation       settled plan
recap / restart pointer     current detailed state
_INDEX                      where to find things
_LAYOUT                     cross-surface structural contract
operator ledgers            precedent for fast-aging shared state
stale-base checks           protection against concurrent overwrite
```

The memory program already created a temporary current-state/resumption pointer expressly so a fresh thread could resume without reconstructing the whole history. The CFW program independently created the same kind of restart pointer.  

The missing piece is one central, compact table answering:

```text
Which programs are open?
Which thread currently occupies each?
What phase is each in?
What is the next gate?
What is each blocked by?
Which repos/files does each intend to write?
Where is the latest settled recap or restart pointer?
```

### Recommended file

```text
/studio/studio ASK/ecology-ASK-EXTERNAL/
ecology-ASK_program-ledger.md
```

The live `_INDEX` should contain only a stable pointer to that ledger—not mirror every program’s state. That preserves the index’s own “map, not a mirror” contract. 

The live `_LAYOUT` should name the ledger as a root operator-state canonical and require ecology execution threads to read it before claiming a write aperture. `_LAYOUT` already owns the cross-surface structural contract and already recognizes root trackers and operator ledgers as distinct from repo authority. 

## What it would solve

### 1. Earlier collision detection

Each program declares only its intended write aperture:

```text
repos / branches
operator canonicals
shared state files such as _INDEX
current review object
```

A new thread compares its intended targets against the open-program ledger before mutating anything. This does not replace worktree and branch checks; it gives an earlier warning.

The four PTXs show why that matters: each long-lived thread has accumulated separate plans, gates, branches, operator writes, blockers, and resumptions.    

### 2. Fresh-thread recovery

ASK could say:

```text
resume ECO-CFW
```

The fresh thread would read:

```text
program ledger
→ plan of record
→ latest recap / restart pointer
→ live canonical and repo owners
```

The program survives thread rollover. The thread is merely its current occupant.

### 3. Less manual cat-herding

ASK would no longer need to remember which thread contains which body of work or reconstruct the remaining sequence after context exhaustion. The program row would point to the latest recap, and each recap would point to its predecessor for history.

## What it must **not** become

This should not be a universal todo system.

A program earns a row only when:

```text
ASK has adopted a folded plan
more than one bounded unit remains
the work may outlive a thread or wait on dependencies
a close condition exists
```

Single PRs, one-off fixes, ordinary review packets, and individual TBIs remain ordinary task or artifact state.

It must also preserve these separations:

```text
program state   ≠ TBI feed-obligation state
program state   ≠ PTX provenance
program state   ≠ repo / branch truth
program state   ≠ Stage-1 review-object identity
```

The shared protocol already separates normative metadata from live operator state: the manifest owns rules and applicability; the operator ledger owns installed state. The proposed program ledger follows that established pattern.

## How much engineering?

Approximate planning estimate:

```text
Manual MVP
  schema + three-file proposal       2–4 hours
  census and seed four programs      2–4 hours
  review and bounded corrections     ordinary operator-canonical Stage-1 cycle

Total
  roughly half a day to one day
```

The **optional** later validator—required fields, valid statuses, resolving pointers, conflicting write apertures—would likely add another one or two bounded days.

The eventual automated TBI/todo system is a materially larger project. The current control-surface architecture explicitly says it does not contain a general automation, synchronization, generator, or CI framework beyond bounded local validators.

That later system would need event semantics, write authority, connector reliability, failure recovery, and lifecycle testing. It should remain separate.

## Recommendation

Proceed with the manual ledger, but do not interrupt the current four programs to build a management platform around them.

```text
YES  one root program ledger
YES  one _INDEX pointer
YES  one _LAYOUT startup-read contract
YES  seed the four current programs
YES  latest recap / restart pointer per program
YES  declared write apertures and next gates

NO   automation
NO   TBI integration
NO   shared-protocol change
NO   consumer propagation
NO   per-update snapshots
NO   daily recap ceremony
NO   task-by-task tracking
```

Program recaps should be event-triggered only:

```text
phase completion
material resequencing
new or cleared blocker
context rollover
program closure
```

The memo includes the proposed ledger schema, lifecycle, collision rules, engineering estimate, implementation sequence, and a relay-ready prompt for a fresh ecology thread. The advisor remains non-writing; implementation still routes through the executor under ASK’s authorization.

## Direct answer: almost no engineering

**This is a protocol change wearing an engineering question's clothes.**

```text
code / automation           zero
new tooling                 zero
shared-protocol change      zero at v1 — ecology-local first
build                       ONE bounded unit, one Stage-1 packet:
                              _PROGRAMS.md (~4-6 KB) + 1 _LAYOUT row +
                              1 _INDEX row + 1 bootstrap line + 1 session-start line
recurring                   one row edit per program event, riding bounded
                            operations that already write an artifact anyway
```

The decisive fact: **CFW just executed the entire proposed lifecycle manually, in this thread.** Ratified plan → folded recap → restart pointer → closure record for the precursor. Every artifact class your registry needs already exists and already gets maintained. What's missing is only **a durable address where a thread discovers those artifacts exist** — today that address is you, which is exactly the cat-herding you're describing.

## The shape

One new root carrier, `_PROGRAMS.md`, sibling to `_INDEX`/`_LAYOUT`/`_BOOTSTRAP`. Separate file rather than an `_INDEX` section for an aging-rate reason: `_INDEX` should age slowly and is already the most contended carrier in the surface (moved three times today); a registry is a state tracker *by design*. Rows are pointer-only — plan of record, latest recap, prior recaps, next gate, owning thread — never restated state, so the row can't drift from the memos it points at. **The row update rides the same bounded operation that writes the event's artifact**, exactly as closure-and-rename are one operation today. No new cadence, no polling.

Both your benefits fall out of one added required-read line: threads read `_PROGRAMS.md` at session start → parallel awareness; fresh thread reads the row → picks up from its pointers, no reconstruction.

## Three guards worth having before ratifying

- **A row is not authorization.** A fresh thread reading a row has *discovered* work, not inherited permission — your feed envelope still governs. Without this guard the registry quietly becomes a self-authorizing queue, which is the failure your future automation step would inherit.
- **Not a second TBI system.** `-TBI` tracks feed obligations on artifacts; this tracks multi-phase programs. Different axes, never crossed.
- **Staleness is the failure mode.** A registry that's 80% current is worse than none because it reads authoritative. Pointer-only rows plus updates-ride-bounded-ops is the mitigation; live canonicals always win over the row.

## One coordination point

The build unit touches `_LAYOUT` and `_INDEX` — and **ecology-35 owns protocol housekeeping and is live**. Where the unit runs is your call (35, a fresh thread, or after 35 settles) — not ecology-36, whose remaining obligation is P2a assembly and whose context is deep. Note this is a genuine `_LAYOUT` edit — the fixed-entrypoints block lists exactly three files today and this adds a fourth contractual locator — unlike CFW's NOT REQUIRED disposition.

Recommendation: fold this with GPT's counterpart as usual, seed the registry with **PRG-001 CFW** plus only what you name as open, run it manually for 2–3 program lifecycles, and only then consider promoting it to shared protocol. Automation stays down the road, and the stable `PRG-nnn` IDs keep that door open at zero present cost.

## Program-registry fold

The folded recommendation adopts Claude’s `_PROGRAMS.md` architecture over the first GPT memo’s longer filename proposal:

```text
_BOOTSTRAP-ecology-ASK.md
_INDEX-ecology-ASK.md
_LAYOUT.md
_PROGRAMS.md
```

`_PROGRAMS.md` becomes the fourth fixed structural entrypoint. It is a compact, fast-aging coordination carrier—not repo truth, a TBI queue, or an authorization surface.

The program lifecycle is now first-class:

```text
ACTIVE
  OPEN
  BLOCKED
  PAUSED

TERMINAL
  COMPLETED
  ABSORBED
  ABORTED
  SUPERSEDED
```

Once a program enters the registry, it must leave through a named terminal disposition. It never disappears because its thread ended, its context filled, or nobody remembered to update it.

The integrity rule is:

```text
zero unexplained active rows

every active row carries:
  owning thread or NONE
  plan of record
  latest state / recap
  current phase
  next gate
  next ASK decision
  declared write aperture
  observed_at
  stale trigger

every terminal row carries:
  disposition
  closure record
  terminal date
  successor / absorbing owner where applicable
```

## CFW fold: Claude → subagents is the mundane prototype

The updated CFW recap now distinguishes two worked examples.

### Prototype A — the workflow already in use

```text
ASK
  authorizes the program objective and governing aperture
      ↓
CLAUDE PARENT EXECUTOR
  receives that intent
  decomposes it into bounded task intent
  feeds scope, constraints, evidence rules, and return format to subagents
      ↓
SUBAGENTS
  form local plans
  search, compare, test, and return findings inside the aperture
      ↓
CLAUDE PARENT EXECUTOR
  verifies every finding
  rejects or integrates the output
  retains synthesis and closure
      ↓
ASK
  authorizes operative writes and subsequent gates
```

This establishes that a computational agent can:

```text
feed delegated intent
set bounded subgoals for lower computational layers
allocate local discretion
recursively delegate work
```

It does not establish that Claude or a subagent occupies the root source-of-intent role.

```text
Claude parent   delegation conduit + bounded intermediate intent-setting
subagent        delegated execution + bounded discretion
human ASK       authorization apex and root SoI for system-ASK
```

The compact guard is:

```text
delegation depth ≠ source-role height
feeding delegated intent ≠ authoring root standing intent
```

### Prototype B — AP image approval

The AP agent is the domain-specific version of the same topology: it evaluates images against an inherited brief and may approve or reject within its aperture.

```text
subagent task judgment
AP image approval
```

are both lower-stack examples of delegated computational discretion. Neither answers whether a computational office can authorize the bounds themselves.

Subagents may form local plans and exercise discretion inside the supplied aperture; the parent verifies and integrates their outputs, while ASK retains operative authorization. This is recursive delegated intent and bounded discretion, not evidence that either computational layer occupies the root source-of-intent role. `delegation depth ≠ source-role height`.

ASK >>

basically we are saying that delegating SoI to subagents is bounded discretion, not SoI?

subagents follow the bounded generativity topology? SoI is the apex, and then the execution span may have bounded discretion (human middle), or variance (generative middle). so subagents is a case of the generative middle?

///

GPT >>

**Yes—with two important corrections.**

## 1. It is not “delegating source of intent”

It is:

```text
source of intent
  → delegates intent, scope, and decision rights
  → to an execution layer
```

The source role itself remains at the apex unless the lower office is separately constituted to hold the complete source-role bundle:

```text
intent
+ legitimate authority
+ locatable closure / recourse
```

A subagent ordinarily receives a task, constraints, and latitude. It does not receive authority to redefine the governing purpose, enlarge its own scope, or authorize the basic standard under which it operates. So:

```text
delegated intent-setting       yes
bounded subgoal-setting        yes
bounded discretion             sometimes
source-of-intent occupancy     no
```

The normative-apex doctrine defines source of intent as the governing role that supplies or authorizes purpose and the standard. Candidate generation—even candidate standards—does not constitute adoption or authorship of that standard.

A more precise sentence is:

> **ASK is not delegating SoI to Claude or the subagents. ASK is delegating intent and bounded authority from the SoI.**

## 2. Subagents are in the “middle,” but not necessarily only the generative middle

At the overall system level, yes:

```text
ASK / SOURCE OF INTENT
  supplies or authorizes purpose, standard, and aperture
        ↓
BOUNDED REALIZATION FIELD — “THE MIDDLE”
  Claude parent
  subagents
  models
  tools
  evidence
  critics
        ↓
SELECTION + CLOSURE
        ↓
GOVERNED RESULT
```

But the middle is not divided as:

```text
human middle          = discretion
computational middle  = variance
```

That would be the wrong axis.

The actual distinction is functional:

```text
variance-producing span
  produces candidates without evaluating and binding them

judgment-bearing span
  an authorized decision-maker evaluates alternatives against the standard
  and binds within scope
```

Either span may be human or computational. The doctrine explicitly allows a human or computational decision-maker to exercise authorized judgment inside the realization field.

So:

> **Subagents occupy the bounded realization field. What role they play inside it depends on their grant.**

## The current Claude → subagent workflow

The topology is nested:

```text
ASK — root SoI
  authorizes the program objective and governing aperture
        ↓
Claude parent executor
  decomposes that objective
  creates bounded sub-briefs
  delegates them to subagents
        ↓
subagents
  search, generate, compare, test, critique, or propose
        ↓
Claude parent
  verifies their findings
  rejects false findings
  selects and integrates usable material
        ↓
advisor review
        ↓
ASK authorization
        ↓
Claude performs the authorized write
```

Your instruction already records Claude→subagent delegation as the primary ASK-P3 worked instance, with AP image approval as the domain-specific instance.  The live P2a restart pointer now carries that prototype and the guard:

```text
delegation depth ≠ source-role height
feeding delegated intent ≠ authoring root standing intent
```



## Where each participant sits

### ASK

```text
root source of intent
supplies / authorizes the purpose and governing standard
controls operative authorization
retains ultimate answerability
```

### Claude parent

Claude is primarily a **delegation conduit and bounded intermediate intent-setter**:

```text
receives ASK-authorized intent
derives bounded subgoals
defines subagent apertures
evaluates returned material
selects what enters the proposed synthesis
```

Those acts can involve bounded discretion. Claude is not merely producing random variance.

But Claude does not independently authorize the root objective or revise ASK’s standing intent. Its local briefs are derived from the upstream standard.

### Ordinary research or critique subagent

Most of the current subagents are primarily **candidate-producing spans**:

```text
search
extract
compare
generate distinctions
identify objections
return findings
```

Even where their work is intelligent and evaluative, their conclusion remains a candidate until the parent verifies and admits it. They normally cannot bind the program, mutate the canonical, or authorize a change.

So, in the ordinary workflow:

```text
subagent output  = generative / analytical candidate field
Claude parent    = verification + local selection / integration
ASK              = operative authorization and apex closure
```

### An explicitly binding computational agent

An AP image-approval agent is different. If it is authorized to evaluate images against a governing standard and bind approval within a named scope, it occupies a **judgment-bearing bounded-discretion span**.

That still does not make it the source of intent:

```text
may approve inside the brief
≠ may author or replace the brief

may bind within its aperture
≠ may authorize the aperture

may exercise discretion
≠ owns ultimate recourse
```

Governance doctrine defines delegated discretion exactly this way: bounded judgment exercised by an authorized decision-maker within the governing standard and delegated scope.

## The recursive topology

Each subagent call is itself a small bounded-generativity bracket nested inside the larger one:

```text
SYSTEM LEVEL

ASK's standing intent / governing standard
  ↓
Claude + subagents realization field
  ↓
ASK-governed closure


NESTED SUBTASK LEVEL

Claude's derived sub-brief
  ↓
subagent realization
  ↓
Claude verifies / selects / integrates
```

At the nested level, Claude temporarily performs a **local legislative function** by specifying the sub-brief—but only under delegated authority from the root grammar. The subagent fills that local aperture. Claude performs local selection over what returns.

That recursion does not create a new apex because the local sub-brief remains derivable from and bounded by the upstream standard.

## The clean formula

```text
ASK                    root SoI

Claude parent          delegation conduit
                        + bounded intermediate intent-setting
                        + local judgment / selection

subagents              bounded realization field
                        usually candidate-producing
                        sometimes judgment-bearing if explicitly authorized

AP approval agent      bounded discretion with local binding authority

none of the above      becomes root SoI merely through deeper delegation
```

So the direct answer is:

> **Yes, subagents are cases inside the bounded-generativity middle. But they are not necessarily only “variance.” A subagent may produce candidate variance, exercise bounded judgment, or both. What it cannot do merely by being delegated a task is become the source of intent.**

## BLOCKING 1 — “delegation recurses freely” contradicts grant-bounded redelegation

`normative-apex.md` currently proposes:

> “Delegation recurses freely…”

But `governance.md` correctly says:

> “A delegate may redelegate only what its own delegation allows…”

In a doctrine section about authority, **freely** can be read as a permission claim: any delegate may recursively dispatch work merely because delegation is structurally possible. That contradicts the attenuation rule being landed in the same patch.

The credible failure is a future executor or system designer treating recursive dispatch capability as an implicit redelegation right—the exact `power ≠ authority` collapse this doctrine rejects.

### Required correction

Replace the sentence opening with:

```markdown
Delegation may recurse where the grant permits: each authorized dispatch opens
a nested aperture inside the outer one, and recursion increases execution depth,
never source-role height.
```

The intended claim remains:

```text
recursion is structurally available
≠
every delegate is authorized to recurse
```

## BLOCKING 2 — the operational chain still skips the program-level authorization act

The proposed `source-of-intent.md` chain ends:

```text
parent verification + selection + integration
  >> authorized program result
```

That visually implies that parent integration itself produces an authorized result. The prose below correctly says local completion is not program closure, but the main operational chain—the most reusable and likely quoted object—omits the distinct final act.

The credible failure is precisely the one ASK wants eliminated:

```text
Claude verifies and integrates
→ therefore the result is operative
```

rather than:

```text
Claude verifies and integrates
→ the governing role authorizes / closes
→ the result becomes operative
```

The section’s opening sentence also says every link “changes what the intent is,” which is broader than the intended claim. A grant, brief, plan, action, and result are not all new versions of the standing intent; they are forms and realizations derived under it.

### Required correction

Use:

```markdown
Standing intent reaches execution through a chain of derivation and realization.
Each link changes its operational form or granularity without changing its
normative origin:
```

Then change the chain’s final steps to:

```text
          >> parent verification + selection + integration
            >> program-level authorization + closure by the governing role
              >> authorized program result
```

This makes explicit:

```text
subtask closure       ≠ program closure
parent integration    ≠ operative authorization
```

## BLOCKING 3 — the new `dispatched` value still falls outside Axis C’s own question

`intent-artifacts.md` currently defines Axis C as:

> “How does it enter an active surface?”

The patch then adds:

```text
dispatched    supplied to a nested execution context …
```

and later says dispatch:

```text
crosses no surface boundary
```

So the new value does not answer the axis’s declared question.

The placement table also uses:

```text
artifact of intent — usually an utterance event
```

inside the **form** column. `artifact of intent` is the family; `utterance event` is the Axis‑A form. The table therefore mixes two classification levels in the exact ontology whose purpose is to keep axes separate.

It additionally folds the durable-prompt variant into the task-brief row even though the existing `prompt canonical` row already owns that form and lineage.

The credible failure is downstream taxonomy drift:

```text
artifact family mistaken for form
nested-context dispatch mistaken for surface activation
canonical prompt mistaken for its dispatched task event
```

### Required correction

Change the Axis‑C question to:

```markdown
**Axis C — activation.** What is its activation posture or event?
```

Keep the new value:

```text
dispatched    supplied to a nested execution context by a delegate acting
              within its grant — no apex relay per dispatch
```

Make the new placement-table row classify only the runtime task event:

```text
subagent task       utterance event           dispatched    none — event
brief
```

Then revise the explanatory paragraph to say:

```markdown
The dispatched sub-brief is ordinarily an utterance event. Where a reusable
durable prompt canonical supplies or generates that brief, the canonical remains
classified by the existing `prompt canonical` row — invocable operative carrier,
canonical lineage — while the resulting sub-brief is the dispatched event.
```

That restores all three distinctions:

```text
artifact family      ≠ form
prompt canonical     ≠ task dispatch
surface activation   ≠ nested-context activation
```

## The three blockers are cleared

### 1. Recursive delegation is now grant-conditioned

The corrected apex language says:

> Delegation may recurse **where the grant permits**; each **authorized** dispatch opens a nested aperture.

That now agrees with governance’s attenuation rule: a delegate may redelegate only what its own grant permits. The doctrine no longer risks converting structural recursion into a free-standing permission to redelegate.

The resulting distinction is clear:

```text
recursion is structurally available
≠
every delegate is authorized to recurse
```

The rest of the apex section remains sound: delegated intent, apertures, and bounded rights travel down the stack; the source role does not travel merely because the execution chain becomes deeper. 

### 2. The program-level authorization act is explicit

The operational chain now ends:

```text
parent verification + selection + integration
  >> program-level authorization + closure by the governing role
    >> authorized program result
```

This eliminates the earlier implication that parent integration alone makes a result operative.

The opening also correctly says the chain changes intent’s **operational form or granularity**, not “what the intent is.” A task brief, local plan, execution grant, and result are derivations or realizations under standing intent; they are not serial replacements for that standing intent. 

The chain now structurally carries both required distinctions:

```text
subtask closure      ≠ program closure
parent integration   ≠ operative authorization
```

### 3. The artifact ontology now classifies at one level per axis

Axis C now asks:

> What is its activation posture or event?

That correctly accommodates an internal nested-context event that crosses no operating-surface boundary.

The runtime task row is now:

```text
subagent task brief   utterance event   dispatched   none — event
```

So the table no longer mixes:

```text
artifact family
with
Axis-A form
```

The durable-carrier distinction is also now explicit:

```text
reusable prompt canonical
  invocable operative carrier
  canonical lineage

resulting sub-brief
  utterance event
  dispatched activation
  no independent lineage
```

That preserves:

```text
artifact family    ≠ form
prompt canonical   ≠ dispatched task event
surface routing    ≠ nested-context dispatch
```



## Substantive review

The complete five-document fold now says the same thing at each altitude.

### Normative apex

```text
delegated intent             ≠ delegated source role
delegation depth             ≠ source-role height
proximate task instruction   ≠ origination of standing intent
```

A lower office becomes a source-of-intent role only if governance separately constitutes or recognizes it for a named scope with attributable purpose, authorization, closure, and answerability. Ordinary dispatch does not perform that constitution.

Neither held question is answered:

* whether a computational office may occupy the source role;
* whether constituted authority must ultimately terminate in natural persons.

That restraint preserves the original ecology‑36 problem rather than settling it by schema. 

### Runtime realization

The operational doctrine now distinguishes:

```text
standing intent
→ execution grant
→ derived task intent
→ dispatched sub-brief
→ local realization
→ parent verification / integration
→ governing-role authorization / closure
→ authorized result
```

Local subgoals remain beneath standing intent. Evidence that the standard should change routes as a candidate correction; it is not silently adopted as a task-level revision.

### Bounded generativity

The nested-bracket model is coherent:

```text
OUTER BRACKET
  apex grammar
  → parent + subagents realization field
  → apex-governed closure

NESTED BRACKET
  derived sub-brief
  → subagent realization
  → parent verification / selection / integration
```

Actor identity remains orthogonal to function:

```text
human or computational span
  may be candidate-producing
  may be judgment-bearing
  may contain both

grant + binding scope
  determine which
```

A computational subagent that reasons, compares, and recommends—but cannot bind—still produces candidate material. An AP approval agent with an explicit scoped binding grant occupies a judgment-bearing span. Neither becomes source of intent merely through capability or delegation depth.

### Governance

Redelegation cannot exceed the parent grant. Parent verification and integration remain assigned unless governance explicitly reallocates those functions. Local binding authority remains distinct from ultimate recourse.

### Intent artifacts

The sub-brief is now explicitly classified as an artifact of intent carrying derived task intent. Its force comes through delegated authority, its activation is dispatch, and it acquires no routed-handoff lifecycle.

The vocabulary guard also passes:

```text
runtime delegation / task dispatch / sub-brief
  for parent → subagent

feeding
  reserved for the apex-mediated external-payload lifecycle
```

delegation may recurse where the grant permits

Both held questions remain open:

The section neither answers nor structurally prejudges either one.

### Operational source of intent — passes

```text
standing intent
→ execution grant
→ derived task intent
→ sub-brief / dispatch

## The thing worth remembering from this stretch

The residual that just closed existed because a *correct* fold left a *stale* pointer behind. The topology in v3 was right the whole time; only its vocabulary went out of date when doctrine landed underneath it. That's the failure mode a program registry is actually for — not tracking work, but catching the moment a durable artifact stops agreeing with its own upstream owner.

Your question — *"have the recursive delegation updates been reflected in the previous CFW recap and reopening notes, if appropriate?"* — is the one that found it. The `if appropriate` was doing real work: the answer was yes for the pointer, no for the canonical, and *supersede rather than edit* for the frozen recap. Three different treatments, and getting that split wrong in either direction would have cost something — an edited v3 would have destroyed provenance, an unconformed pointer would have reproduced quarantined vocabulary in canonical v2.

Both source-role questions remain deliberately open. Nothing in this program has tilted them, and the landed text says so in its own words.
